He signed long-term endorsement deals with luxury brands [Dolce & Gabbana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolce_&_Gabbana) and [Audemars Piguet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audemars_Piguet),[^cite_note-Fourth-415][^cite_note-417] and became a [brand ambassador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brand_ambassador) for [Gillette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gillette_\(brand\)), [Turkish Airlines](167925.md), [Ooredoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ooredoo), and [Tata Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tata_Motors), among other companies.[^cite_note-422] Messi has also been the face of two video game series: [Konami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konami)'s *[Pro Evolution Soccer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro_Evolution_Soccer)* and [EA Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EA_Sports)' *[FIFA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_\(video_game_series\))*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuinness_World_Records2014102-423][^cite_note-424]



Messi's Argentina jersey, supplied by Adidas, was sold out worldwide during the 2022 World Cup.

[^cite_note-Arg_sales-425]

*[SportsPro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SportsPro)* cited Messi as one of the world's most marketable athletes every year between 2010 and 2015.[^cite_note-426] He became the first footballer to receive his own sub-brand of Adidas boots, the Adidas Messi, in 2015.[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428] The same year, a Barcelona jersey with Messi's name and number was the best-selling replica jersey worldwide.[^cite_note-429] During the 2022 World Cup, Adidas sold out of Messi's No. 10 Argentina jersey worldwide.[^cite_note-Arg_sales-425]

In 2013, a Turkish Airlines advertisement starring Messi and [Kobe Bryant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kobe_Bryant) became the most-watched advertisement on [YouTube](3524766.md), with 137 million views. It was voted by users as the best advertisement of the decade 2005–2015.[^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431] In June 2021, Messi signed a five-year deal to become a brand ambassador for [Hard Rock Cafe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_Rock_Cafe),[^cite_note-432] and the following year he became a tourism ambassador for [Saudi Arabia](349303.md). He was criticised for partnering with Saudi Arabia because of the country's poor record on human rights.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434]

### Public art



[Street art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_art) depicting Messi in [Barcelona](4443.md)

[Street art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_art) and public [murals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural) depicting Messi have been painted around the world.[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436] One prominent work of art is the *[Sistine Chapel of Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sistine_Chapel_of_Football)*, a parody of [Michelangelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelangelo)'s *[The Creation of Adam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Creation_of_Adam)* that features Messi, [Diego Maradona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diego_Maradona) and several other Argentine footballers. The work is exhibited at the Sportivo Pereyra club in [Barracas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barracas,_Buenos_Aires), [Buenos Aires](255919.md).[^cite_note-437]

[Madame Tussauds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madame_Tussauds) unveiled a wax sculpture of Messi at [Wembley Stadium](8913012.md) in 2012.[^cite_note-438] Several days after Messi announced his retirement from [Argentina's national team](454699.md) in June 2016, a bronze statue of him was erected in [Buenos Aires](255919.md) in an attempt to convince him to return.[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440] Following Argentina's 2022 World Cup victory, a life-sized statue of Messi holding the World Cup trophy was unveiled outside the [CONMEBOL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CONMEBOL) headquarters in [Luque, Paraguay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luque,_Paraguay) in March 2023. The statue of Messi stands alongside statues of [Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pel%C3%A9) and Maradona.[^cite_note-441] In December 2025, during Messi's [tour of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lionel_Messi's_2025_India_Tour), a 70-foot (21 m) statue of him holding the World Cup trophy was unveiled at the Sree Bhumi Sporting Club in [Lake Town, Kolkata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Town,_Kolkata).[^cite_note-442] The statue was later removed due to instability. In June 2026, an 85-foot (26 m) [statue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cutral_Co_Messi_Statue) of Messi was erected in [Cutral Có](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cutral_C%C3%B3), Argentina.[^cite_note-443]

### Media

*[Messi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Messi_\(2014_film\))*, a documentary directed by [Álex de la Iglesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81lex_de_la_Iglesia), premiered at the [71st Venice International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/71st_Venice_International_Film_Festival) in August 2014.[^cite_note-444] *[Messi's World Cup: The Rise of a Legend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Messi's_World_Cup:_The_Rise_of_a_Legend)*, a biographical docuseries focusing on Messi's career, his highs and lows with the Argentina national football team, and their eventual success at the 2022 FIFA World Cup, aired on [Apple TV+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_TV+) on 21 February 2024.[^cite_note-445]

### Business ventures

On 4 June 2024, Messi announced the release of [Más+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%A1s+), an American brand of [sports drinks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_drink) and [energy drinks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_drink).[^cite_note-SafidDeen2024-446] Messi created the drinks because he could not find a flavourful and healthy hydration option that suited his needs.[^cite_note-449] On 19 September, Messi launched the production company 525 Rosario, which produces films, sporting events coverage and branded commercials for athletes worldwide. It is a joint venture with Smuggler Entertainment, which co-produced *Messi's World Cup: The Rise of a Legend* earlier in 2024.[^cite_note-450] In 2025, Messi was invited by his [Inter Miami](41853326.md) teammate [Luis Suárez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Su%C3%A1rez_\(Uruguayan_footballer\)) to play a role in [Deportivo LSM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deportivo_LSM), the Uruguayan professional football club that Suárez founded.[^cite_note-451] On 16 April 2026, Messi acquired full ownership of the Spanish football club [UE Cornellà](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UE_Cornell%C3%A0).[^cite_note-452]

## Philanthropy

Throughout his career, Messi has been involved in charitable efforts aimed at children, a commitment that stems in part from the medical difficulties he faced in his own childhood. Since 2004, he has contributed his time and finances to the [United Nations Children's Fund](19698110.md) (UNICEF), an organisation that has also collaborated with [FC Barcelona](68187.md).[^cite_note-Leo_Messi:_Goodwill_Ambassador-453][^cite_note-454] Messi has served as a [UNICEF Goodwill Ambassador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNICEF_Goodwill_Ambassador) since 2010; he completed his first field mission for the organisation that year as he travelled to Haiti to bring awareness to the plight of the country's children following the [2010 Haiti earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Haiti_earthquake). He has since participated in UNICEF campaigns related to [HIV prevention](31330718.md), education, and the social inclusion of disabled children.[^cite_note-455] In November 2013, Messi and his one-year-old son Thiago were part of a publicity campaign to raise awareness of mortality rates among disadvantaged children.[^cite_note-456]

In 2007, Messi launched the Leo Messi Foundation, which supports access to health care, education, and sport for children.[^cite_note-457] It was established following Messi's visit to a hospital in Boston for terminally ill children, an experience that resonated with him to the point that he decided to reinvest part of his earnings into society.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBalagué2013576–577-458] Through his foundation, Messi has awarded research grants, financed medical training, and invested in the development of medical centres and projects in Argentina, Spain, and elsewhere in the world.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBalagué2013576–577-458][^cite_note-459] In addition to receiving funds from Messi's own fundraising activities, the foundation receives financial support from various companies which have endorsement agreements with Messi, with Adidas as the main sponsor.[^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461] A gold replica of Messi's left foot, weighing 25 kg (55 lb) and valued at \$5.3 million, went on sale in Japan in 2013 to raise funds for victims of the [2011 Tōhoku earthquake and tsunami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_T%C5%8Dhoku_earthquake_and_tsunami).[^cite_note-462]

Messi has invested in youth football in Argentina. He supports Sarmiento, a football club based in the Rosario neighbourhood where he was born, and in 2013 he committed to the refurbishment of the club's facilities and the installation of all-weather pitches. Messi also financially supports the management of several youth players at [Newell's Old Boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newell's_Old_Boys) (his boyhood club) and rival club [Rosario Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosario_Central), as well as at [River Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Club_Atl%C3%A9tico_River_Plate) and [Boca Juniors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boca_Juniors) in Buenos Aires.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBalagué2013576–577-458] In 2012, Messi funded the construction of a new gymnasium and a dormitory inside Newell's [stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estadio_Marcelo_Bielsa) for their youth academy. His youth coach at Newell's, Ernesto Vecchio, is employed by the Leo Messi Foundation as a talent scout for young players.[^cite_note-Here-42] On 7 June 2016, Messi won a libel case against [*La Razón* newspaper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Raz%C3%B3n_\(Madrid\)) and was awarded €65,000 in damages, which he donated to the charity [Médecins Sans Frontières](20498.md).[^cite_note-463] During the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), Messi and his FC Barcelona teammates announced they would take a 70% salary cut to offset the club's financial losses, and said they would make further contributions to ensure club employees received full salaries while unable to work.[^cite_note-464] Messi also donated €1 million to fight the spread of COVID-19.[^cite_note-465][^cite_note-466]

In November 2016, while the [Argentine Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_Football_Association) was being run by a FIFA committee due to an economic crisis, three of the national team's security staff told Messi that they had not received their salaries for six months. He paid the salaries of the three employees.[^cite_note-467][^cite_note-468] In February 2021, Messi made a donation to the [Museu Nacional d'Art de Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museu_Nacional_d'Art_de_Catalunya): the Adidas shoes he wore when he scored his 644th goal for Barcelona and broke [Pelé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pel%C3%A9)'s record for most goals scored for a single club. The museum later auctioned off the shoes for £125,000 to support a charity focused on children with cancer.[^cite_note-469]

Ahead of the [2021 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica), Messi donated three signed sweatshirts to the Chinese pharmaceutical firm [Sinovac Biotech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinovac_Biotech) – whose directors spoke of their admiration for Messi – in order to secure 50,000 doses of Sinovac's [COVID-19 vaccine](63319438.md), [CoronaVac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CoronaVac), in the hope of vaccinating all South American football players. The plan, which was brokered by Uruguay's president [Luis Lacalle Pou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Lacalle_Pou), caused some controversy for its focus on football players, given widespread vaccine scarcity on the continent.[^cite_note-Vac-470] In January 2025, Messi was awarded the [Presidential Medal of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom), the highest civilian award of the United States, for his contributions to healthcare and education programs for children around the world.[^cite_note-PMOF-471][^cite_note-472]

On 4 August 2026, Messi donated €80,000 to support reconstruction efforts in the [Sierra Oeste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sierra_Oeste?action=edit\&redlink=1) region of the [Community of Madrid](69802.md) following the [wildfires](83729920.md). [Isabel Díaz Ayuso](59693424.md), President of the Community of Madrid, publicly thanked him for the donation.[^cite_note-473]

## Personal life

### Family and relationships

Since 2008, Messi has been in a relationship with [Antonela Roccuzzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonela_Roccuzzo), whom he married on 30 June 2017 in their home city of [Rosario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosario,_Santa_Fe).[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-475] Roccuzzo is the cousin of Messi's childhood best friend, [Lucas Scaglia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Scaglia), and Messi has known her since he was five years old.[^cite_note-476] After keeping their relationship private for a year, Messi confirmed their romance in an interview in January 2009.[^cite_note-477] Messi and Roccuzzo have three sons, born in 2012, 2015, and 2018.[^cite_note-478] To celebrate Roccuzzo's first pregnancy, Messi placed the ball under his shirt after scoring in Argentina's 4–0 win against Ecuador on 2 June 2012, before confirming the pregnancy in an interview two weeks later.[^cite_note-479] Messi and his family are [Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic).[^cite_note-480]

Messi has a close relationship with his immediate family members, particularly his mother, Celia, whose face is tattooed on his left shoulder. His professional affairs are largely run as a family business. His father [Jorge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Messi) was his agent and manager from 2000 until 2026, when he died at the age of 68.[^cite_note-481] His oldest brother, Rodrigo, handles his daily schedule and publicity. His mother and other brother, Matías, manage his charitable organisation, the Leo Messi Foundation, and take care of personal and professional matters in Rosario.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBalagué201331,_143,_570-482] His younger sister, María Sol, had a leadership role at Messi's fashion brand The Messi Store before joining his production company 525 Rosario.[^cite_note-siblings-35]

Since leaving for Spain at age 13, Messi has maintained close ties to Rosario, and has preserved his [Rosarino accent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rioplatense_Spanish). He has retained ownership of his family's old house, although it has long stood empty, and he maintains a penthouse apartment in an exclusive residential building for his mother, as well as a family compound just outside the city. Messi keeps in frequent contact with a small group of confidants in Rosario, most of whom were fellow members of "The Machine of '87" at [Newell's Old Boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newell's_Old_Boys). He was on bad terms with the club after his transfer to Barcelona, but by 2012 their public feud had ended, with Newell's embracing their ties with Messi and issuing a club membership card to his newborn son.[^cite_note-484] Messi has long planned to end his playing career at Newell's.[^cite_note-485] Messi has citizenship in Argentina, Italy, and Spain.[^cite_note-486]

### Tax fraud

Messi came under investigation in 2013 for suspected [tax evasion](2256795.md) in Spain. Offshore companies in [tax havens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_haven) such as [Uruguay](31843.md), [Switzerland](26748.md) and [Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belize) were used to evade €4.1 million in taxes related to his sponsorship earnings between 2007 and 2009. Messi, who pleaded ignorance to the scheme, voluntarily paid arrears of €5.1 million in August 2013. On 6 July 2016, Messi and his father were both found guilty of tax fraud and were given [suspended](711640.md) 21-month prison sentences and ordered to pay €1.7 million and €1.4 million in fines, respectively.[^cite_note-487] In court, Messi told the judge, "I just played football. I signed the contracts because I trusted my dad and the lawyers and we had decided that they would take charge of those things." An unrelated [shell company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shell_company) in Panama set up in 2012 was subsequently identified as belonging to the Messis in the [Panama Papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panama_Papers) data leak.[^cite_note-488]

[^cite_note-U23-2]: &#x20;Does not include an [unofficial friendly match](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-FIFA_international_football) played on 24 May 2008 in Barcelona between Argentina U23 and the [Catalonia national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catalonia_national_football_team),<sup>[\[471\]](#cite_note-523)</sup><sup>[\[472\]](#cite_note-524)</sup> as Catalonia is not affiliated with either [FIFA](11049.md) or [UEFA](32332.md) as a [national member association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_men's_national_association_football_teams) and is therefore not allowed to participate in official competitions.<sup>[\[473\]](#cite_note-525)</sup>

[^cite_note-7]: &#x20;Spanish: [\[ljoˈnel anˈdɾes ˈleo ˈmesi\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Spanish). According to FCBarcelona.com, and his authorised biography, *Messi* by [Guillem Balagué](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillem_Balagu%C3%A9), his surname is "Messi", in accordance with Argentine customs.<sup>[\[2\]](#cite_note-BarcaProfile-3)</sup><sup>[\[3\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBalagué201332–37-4)</sup> Other sources, including a 2014 document by [FIFA](11049.md), state his surname as "Messi Cuccittini".<sup>[\[4\]](#cite_note-5)</sup> After winning a libel case in 2017, Messi's own management company stated: "The football player **Lionel Andrés Messi Cuccittini** has donated a total of €72,783.20 to the organisation [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md)."<sup>[\[5\]](#cite_note-6)</sup>

[^cite_note-11]: &#x20;Attributed to multiple references:<sup>[\[6\]](#cite_note-8)</sup><sup>[\[7\]](#cite_note-9)</sup><sup>[\[8\]](#cite_note-10)</sup>

[^cite_note-BDOFIFA-14]: &#x20;Messi received *[France Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_Football)*'s [2009 Ballon d'Or](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Ballon_d'Or) and [FIFA](11049.md)'s [2009 FIFA World Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_FIFA_World_Player_of_the_Year) award, prior to their [six-year merger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Ballon_d'Or#History); he proceeded to win the newly formed award, the [FIFA Ballon d'Or](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Ballon_d'Or), four times: [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_FIFA_Ballon_d'Or), [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_FIFA_Ballon_d'Or), [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_FIFA_Ballon_d'Or), [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_FIFA_Ballon_d'Or). Both organisations credited him with five (FIFA) Ballon d'Or awards.<sup>[\[C\]](#cite_note-11)</sup> He then went on to win the Ballon d'Or three more times – in [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ballon_d'Or), [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Ballon_d'Or), [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Ballon_d'Or) – again solely assigned by *France Football*, and won the newly established [The Best FIFA Men's Player](52821005.md) three times as well – in [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Best_FIFA_Football_Awards_2019#The_Best_FIFA_Men's_Player), [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Best_FIFA_Football_Awards_2022#The_Best_FIFA_Men's_Player) and [2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Best_FIFA_Football_Awards_2023#The_Best_FIFA_Men's_Player). *France Football* recognises him as a record eight-time Ballon d'Or winner and FIFA recognises him as a record eight-time world's best player.<sup>[\[9\]](#cite_note-12)</sup><sup>[\[10\]](#cite_note-13)</sup>

[^cite_note-TrophiesNote-29]: &#x20;According to [FC Barcelona](68187.md),<sup>[\[11\]](#cite_note-BarcelonaHonours-15)</sup> [FIFA](11049.md),<sup>[\[12\]](#cite_note-FIFAHonours-16)</sup> the [Royal Spanish Football Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Spanish_Football_Federation),<sup>[\[13\]](#cite_note-17)</sup> and multiple media outlets,<sup>[\[14\]](#cite_note-18)</sup><sup>[\[15\]](#cite_note-19)</sup><sup>[\[16\]](#cite_note-20)</sup> Messi also won the [2005 Supercopa de España](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Supercopa_de_Espa%C3%B1a), bringing his Barcelona trophy total to 35.<sup>[\[17\]](#cite_note-21)</sup> However, this particular trophy is not credited here since Messi was out of the squad and did not feature in either of the two matches against [Real Betis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Betis).<sup>[\[18\]](#cite_note-FirstMiami-22)</sup><sup>[\[19\]](#cite_note-23)</sup> Similarly, Messi and [Inter Miami](41853326.md) were awarded a trophy for winning the [Eastern Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Conference_\(MLS\)) title during the [2025 MLS Cup playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_MLS_Cup_playoffs). However, this trophy is not officially recognized by FIFA, so it is not credited here.<sup>[\[20\]](#cite_note-24)</sup><sup>[\[21\]](#cite_note-25)</sup> For the aforementioned reasons, sources disagree on Messi's total number of accolades, ranging from 47 to 49 official career trophies.<sup>[\[22\]](#cite_note-ElGrafico-26)</sup><sup>[\[23\]](#cite_note-27)</sup><sup>[\[24\]](#cite_note-28)</sup>
